| Concern | Owner | Filter's role |
|---|---|---|
| Whether, when and for whom something lies; its kind, life, leak requirement | Sanity | draws what it is told, never decides |
| The true value | Graph, Suit | the only non-diagnostic wrapper |
| The displayed value and its trajectory | **Filter** | owns |
| The needle sprite, digit mechanism, card, settle and tremor | Instruments | supplies `motion_mode` and the value |
| The HUD frame and layout | HUD | feeds the five readings, nothing else |
| The decoy node and its leak and budget | Lighting | publishes the entry content |
| The sound asset and mix | Adaptive Audio | names the class ids and sends requests |
| The horror rig asset and pose | Horror | sets `leak`, substitutes or omits the body, and feeds pose streams only for phantoms (VS-A) and class-(c) substitutions driven by the crewmate's true velocity (HE-40); never poses a real horror |
| The truth that survives | Graph, F&T, Suit, Attention, Layout, Controller | leaves it bit-identical |

## Formulas

*Conventions.* Every formula below is a **presentation** computation. Nothing here is hashed into `state_hash`, replicated or persisted (KERN D.12 excludes the perceived channel), so the kernel's integer-only determinism rule does not bind D.1 to D.13 and D.15 to D.17, which use floating point on the render thread. **D.14 is the one integer-only formula**: its outputs must reproduce across builds and platforms and are pinned by test vectors (AC-PF-30). Angles are degrees on the instrument's dial arc `arc_deg`; dial readings are per mille (`pm`) of the instrument's range, `pm = deg x 1000 / arc_deg`; pixel measures are art px of the 640x360 grid (`n` art px is `n x k` screen px at ladder step `k`). Time is either **sim time** (`T_c`, the last committed tick count, plus `tick_alpha`, KERN D.3) or **`present_time`** (PIPE PR-9, frozen while PAUSED); each formula says which. `[PLACEHOLDER]` marks a number invented here (range and registered-name proposal in Tuning Knobs); a name followed by "(cited)" is owned elsewhere and used at its registry value. The numbering D.1 to D.17 is the one the rules cite: PF-11 D.1, PF-13 D.2 and D.3, PF-17 D.4, PF-15 D.5, PF-14 D.6, PF-21 D.7, PF-30 D.8, PF-18 D.9, PF-31 D.10, PF-32 D.11, PF-42 D.12, D.13 CPU, PF-37 D.14, PF-20 D.15, PF-35 D.16, PF-19 D.17. Dependency formulas are referenced by name, never restated: KERN D.3 `tick_alpha`, KERN D.10 `rng_derive` (sim-only, never called here), KERN D.12 `state_hash`, PIPE D.15 `flash_count`, PIPE D.16 `D_BUDGET`, LO-22 (including `flash_governor`), SN D.5 (onset). GDScript integer overflow semantics, `Dictionary` ordering and per-call costs are not sourceable (there is no `scripting.md` or `core.md` in `docs/engine-reference/godot/`); every place that depends on them is marked `[UNV]`.

### D.1 Lie clock

The lie clock formula (registry: `lie_displayed_value`; covers D.1 to D.4) is defined as:

`lie_tau = max(0, (T_c - SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR * act_run + tick_alpha) / TICK_RATE)`

`first_frame_discrepancy_bound_deg = GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG * (n_f + 1) / TICK_RATE`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T_c` | int | 0 to 2^53 | KERN tick counter | last committed tick; presentation reads it and never writes |
| `SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR` | int | 12 (cited, registered; equal to the kernel hook divisor by relation, not a knob) | Sanity | ticks per sanity run |
| `act_run` | int | >= 0 | `sanity.perception` entry (`active_run` = `start_run` + delay_runs, published by Sanity; registry `sanity.perception`) | the run on which the entry became ACTIVE. Sanity stamps `start_run` at the accept and activates `ONSET_DELAY_RUNS` (10 to 25 runs) later, so `start_run` is **not** the origin. |
| `tick_alpha` | float | [0, 1] | KERN D.3 (cited) | interpolation fraction inside the current tick |
| `TICK_RATE` | int | 60 (cited) | Command & Simulation Layer | ticks per second |
| `n_f` | int | 1 to 2 | measured | ticks that elapse per rendered frame (1 at 60 fps, 2 at the 30 fps floor) |
| `lie_tau` | float s | 0 to about 120.2 | derived | seconds since activation; the upper end is the `LIE_LIFE_S` maximum (120 s, Sanity) plus one run and one frame |
| `GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG` | float deg/s | 2.0 (named here, value from ART 7.3) | art bible | the fastest drift |

**Output Range:** `lie_tau` is 0 at activation and clamped at 0 when a restore or a `force_stage` makes `act_run x 12 > T_c`. It is frozen while PAUSED (the tick and `tick_alpha` are frozen). It is unbounded above only if Sanity fails to remove an expired entry. **First-frame bound:** the first rendered frame sees the entry at most one frame after its hook tick, so `lie_tau <= (n_f + 1) / TICK_RATE` and the displayed-minus-true discrepancy on the first ACTIVE frame is at most `2.0 x 3 / 60 = 0.1 deg` at the 30 fps floor (0.067 deg at 60 fps), which is below `LEAK_EPS_DEG` 0.25 deg: the first frame carries no visible departure (AC-PF-45 tests "below `LEAK_EPS_DEG`", not "exactly zero"). If the Sanity hook's phase offset is not 0, `act_run x 12` is up to 11 ticks (0.18 s) early; the error is constant per lie and invisible.

**Example:** `act_run` 1000, `T_c` 12300, `tick_alpha` 0.5: `lie_tau = (12300 - 12000 + 0.5) / 60 = 5.008 s`. A restore that lands with `T_c` 11990 gives `(11990 - 12000 + 0) / 60 < 0`, clamped to 0. While PAUSED for 30 s `T_c` and `tick_alpha` do not move, so `lie_tau` stays 5.008 s.

### D.2 Glide parameters (rate and magnitude, drawn once per lie)

The glide parameters formula is defined as:

`rate_draw = GLIDE_RATE_MIN_DEG + (GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG - GLIDE_RATE_MIN_DEG) * (h_rate mod 1001) / 1000`

`D_draw = D_DRAW_MIN_DEG + (D_MAX_FRAC_ARC * arc_deg - D_DRAW_MIN_DEG) * (h_mag mod 1001) / 1000` (kind GLIDE); `D_target = D_draw` for GLIDE and `D_target = D_MAX_FRAC_ARC * arc_deg` for FALSE_OK and FALSE_FAIL, whose needle stops at the zone (D.3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

`rate_floor = max(GLIDE_RATE_MIN_DEG, 28.6 / needle_len_px)`

`rate_need = D_target / (GLIDE_REACH_FRAC * L_s)`

`rate_nom = clamp(max(rate_draw, rate_need), rate_floor, GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG)`

`D_eff = min(D_target, GLIDE_REACH_FRAC * rate_nom * L_s)` (independent of `rm` and of the live true value: reduced motion scales the **time axis only**, so it is not a hidden difficulty slider)

`rate = max(RM_RATE_FLOOR_DEG, rm * rate_nom)`, `t_arrive = D_eff / rate`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `h_rate`, `h_mag` | uint32 | 0 to 2^32 - 1 | D.14, tags 2 and 3 | per-lie hash draws of `(viewer, ref_key, lie_key, 0)` (`epoch` is 0 for every per-lie draw, D.14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `GLIDE_RATE_MIN_DEG`, `GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG` | float deg/s | 0.5, 2.0 (named here, values from ART 7.3) | art bible | the drift-rate band (registry: `GLIDE_RATE_BAND`, with `NEVER_REST_MAX_S` and `WANDER_AMP_PX`; it bounds the **drift** component only; the wander is separate, D.4) |
| `D_DRAW_MIN_DEG` | float deg | 24 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 24 to 40 (raised from 12 to 40 so `rm_min x D_DRAW_MIN_DEG >= D_VIS_MIN_DEG` holds across the knob ranges *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*) | Filter | smallest drawn departure |
| `D_MAX_FRAC_ARC` | float | 0.4 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 0.25 to 0.5 | Filter | largest departure as a fraction of the arc |
| `arc_deg` | float deg | 180 to 300 (240 used in examples) | calibration record (PF-45, provisional) | dial sweep |
| `needle_len_px` | float art px | >= 14.3 (`NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX` `[PLACEHOLDER]` 16, eligibility floor 14.3 = `28.6 / GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG`); recommended 24 | calibration record (provisional) | pivot-to-tip length; below 14.3 the needle is ineligible (`28.6 / 14 = 2.04 > GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG`) |
| `28.6` | float | constant | derived | `0.5 x 180 / pi`: the angular rate that moves the tip 0.5 art px per second |
| `GLIDE_REACH_FRAC` | float | 0.6 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 0.4 to 0.8 | Filter | fraction of the lie's life by which the departure must complete |
| `L_s` | float s | 40 to 120 | `(end_run - act_run) x 0.2` from the entry (the `LIE_LIFE_S` range, SN-16) | remaining life from activation (`end_run` = `active_run` + life_runs, registry `sanity.perception`) |
| `rm` | float | 1.0, or `RM_GLIDE_SCALE` 0.7 under reduced motion (range 0.7 to 1.0, *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*); latched per lie at activation and at `hard_start` | PF-39 | reduced-motion scale of the **time axis only** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `RM_RATE_FLOOR_DEG` | float deg/s | 0.3 (PF-39) | Filter | the rate never goes below this under reduced motion |
| `room_deg` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | removed from `D_eff` | n/a | D.3 | the dial stop is a **live clamp** of the target (D.3 `W_M`), evaluated every frame, not a per-lie constant: a constant that read the true value at activation would change after a restore and was evaluated at an unspecified time |
| `rate_nom`, `rate`, `D_eff` | float | `rate_nom` in [`rate_floor`, 2.0]; `rate` in [0.3, 2.0]; `D_eff` in [`min(D_DRAW_MIN_DEG, 48)`, 0.4 x arc_deg] | derived | per-lie constants (identical at every `rm` for `rate_nom` and `D_eff`) |

**Output Range:** `rate_nom` is in `[rate_floor, GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG]` and `rate` is its time-axis image (`rm x rate_nom`, never below 0.3). `D_eff <= GLIDE_REACH_FRAC x rate_nom x L_s <= 0.6 x 2.0 x 120 = 144 deg`, and `D_eff <= D_MAX_FRAC_ARC x arc_deg = 96 deg` at 240; `D_eff >= min(D_DRAW_MIN_DEG, 0.6 x 2.0 x 40) = 24 deg` for GLIDE, so a drawn departure is never below `D_VIS_MIN_DEG` by construction. **Build assertions** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `rm_min x D_DRAW_MIN_DEG >= D_VIS_MIN_DEG` (`D_VIS_MIN_DEG` `[PLACEHOLDER]` 12 deg): `0.7 x 24 = 16.8 >= 12`; and, new *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, the arrival bound `GLIDE_REACH_FRAC / rm_min <= RM_ARRIVE_FRAC_MAX` (`RM_ARRIVE_FRAC_MAX` named here, 0.9 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 0.8 to 0.95): `0.6 / 0.7 = 0.857 <= 0.9`, so `t_arrive(rm_min) <= 0.9 x L_s` and a lie is held at least `ZONE_DWELL_MIN_S := L_s_min x (1 - RM_ARRIVE_FRAC_MAX)` = 4.0 s (5.7 s at defaults) before it expires; at `GLIDE_REACH_FRAC` 0.8 the bound needs `rm_min` 0.89, which is why the two knobs are coupled; `RM_RATE_FLOOR_DEG` 0.3 never binds above `rm` 0.6 (0.7 x 0.5 = 0.35). It is exactly the visible-in-time condition: the slowest nominal rate of a GLIDE lie on the shortest life is `rate_need = D_DRAW_MIN_DEG / (GLIDE_REACH_FRAC x 40 s)` = 1.0 deg/s (the earlier form used `GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG` for `rate_nom` and was loose), so `D_VIS_MIN_DEG / (rm_min x 1.0) <= GLIDE_REACH_FRAC x 40 s` reduces to the same inequality; the knob ranges are tightened to `D_DRAW_MIN_DEG` 24 to 40 and `D_VIS_MIN_DEG` 8 to 12, so it can only fail by a deliberate out-of-range edit. **Reduced motion** stretches only the time: the same lie arrives at `D_eff / (rm x rate_nom)`, up to `1 / rm` times later than the nominal `t_arrive` (so a 40 s GLIDE lie at `rm` 0.7 arrives at 34.3 s, 86 % of its life, 14 deg out after 10 s and 28 deg after 20 s) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and `D_eff`, the zone it can reach and the discrepancy it can show are the same as for a player with reduced motion off. The rate is a function of `D_target` and the hash only, never of the live true value, so it is a per-lie constant and restore-exact.

**Example:** `arc_deg` 240, `needle_len_px` 24 (`rate_floor = max(0.5, 28.6 / 24) = 1.19`), `GLIDE_REACH_FRAC` 0.6, hash `H(1, 0x1234, 7, 0)` of D.14 (viewer 1, `ref_key` 0x1234, `start_run` 7): `h_rate mod 1001` = 61 gives `rate_draw = 0.5 + 1.5 x 0.061 = 0.59`; `h_mag mod 1001` = 566 gives `D_draw = 24 + 72 x 0.566 = 64.75 deg`.

| Case | `L_s` | `rate_need` | `rate_nom` | `D_eff` | `t_arrive` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| stage 1 GLIDE, short life | 40 s | 64.75 / (0.6 x 40) = 2.70 | clamp(max(0.59, 2.70), 1.19, 2.0) = **2.0** | min(64.75, 0.6 x 2.0 x 40 = 48) = **48 deg** | 24 s (0.60 `L_s`) |
| stage 2 GLIDE, mid life | 80 s | 1.35 | **1.35** | 64.75 | 48 s (0.60 `L_s`) |
| stage 3 GLIDE, long life | 120 s | 0.90 | max(0.59, 0.90) raised to `rate_floor` **1.19** | min(64.75, 85.8) = **64.75 deg** | 54.3 s (0.45 `L_s`) |
| stage 1 FALSE_OK, short life (*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*) | 40 s | 96 / (0.6 x 40) = 4.0 | clamp(4.0) = **2.0** | min(96, 48) = **48 deg** | 24 s |
| stage 2 FALSE_OK, mid life | 80 s | 96 / 48 = 2.0 | **2.0** | min(96, 96) = **96 deg** | 48 s |
| stage 3 FALSE_FAIL, long life | 120 s | 96 / 72 = 1.33 | **1.33** | min(96, 0.6 x 1.33 x 120 = 96) = **96 deg** | 72 s |

The short-life row shows the reach rule working: a 64.75-deg draw would need 2.7 deg/s, so the departure is reduced to what 2.0 deg/s can do in `GLIDE_REACH_FRAC` of the life (counter `lies_partial` measures how often this happens; OQ-PF-10). Under reduced motion (`rm` 0.7) the six rows keep every `D_eff` and arrive at `D_eff / (0.7 x rate_nom)`: the three GLIDE rows at 34.3 s, 68.6 s and 77.6 s and the three FALSE rows at 34.3 s, 68.6 s and 102.9 s (each at 0.86 `L_s` or less; the hold before expiry is 5.7 s, 11.4 s and 42.4 s on the GLIDE rows and 5.7 s, 11.4 s and 17.1 s on the FALSE rows); no discrepancy is smaller, only later *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

### D.3 Displayed value (the lie trajectory and the slew)

The displayed value formula (registry: `lie_displayed_value`; covers D.1 to D.4) is defined as:

`delta_pm(tau) = min(rate * tau, D_eff) * 1000 / arc_deg`

`raw_pm(t) = u_true(t) + s * delta_pm(lie_tau)` for kind GLIDE

`raw_pm(t) = u_true(t) + sgn(u_aim(t) - u_true(t)) * min(delta_pm(lie_tau), abs(u_aim(t) - u_true(t)))` for kinds FALSE_OK and FALSE_FAIL, with the aim point `u_aim(t) = clamp(u_true(t), z_lo + m_z, z_hi - m_z)` (the zone centre if the zone is narrower than `2 m_z`), `m_z = max(ZONE_MARGIN_PM, W_M)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

`target_pm(t) = clamp(raw_pm(t), W_M, 1000 - W_M)` (the live dial clamp; `W_M` is the wander peak in pm)

`slew_pm(t + dt) = clamp(slew_pm(t) + clamp(target_pm(t + dt) - slew_pm(t), -R_act * dt, +R_act * dt), u_true - DISC_CAP_PM, u_true + DISC_CAP_PM)`, `R_act = rm * GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG * 1000 / arc_deg`, `DISC_CAP_PM = D_MAX_FRAC_ARC * 1000`, `hard_start: slew_pm := clamp(target_pm, u_true - DISC_CAP_PM, u_true + DISC_CAP_PM)`

`displayed_pm(t) = clamp(round(slew_pm(t) + w_pm(t)), 0, 1000)`, `displayed_value = value_lo + (value_hi - value_lo) * displayed_pm / 1000`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `u_true(t)` | float pm | [0, 1000] | the Filter's own read of `true_value` mapped through the calibration record (the Filter is the only non-diagnostic caller, LS-19) | the live true reading |
| `s` | int | {-1, +1} | the kind's side for FALSE_*; hash tag 8 for GLIDE, comfort-biased per `KIND_W` (Sanity-owned) | departure direction on the dial |
| `[z_lo, z_hi]` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | float pm | within [0, 1000] | calibration record: the OK (FALSE_OK) or alarm (FALSE_FAIL) zone as an interval | the zone a FALSE_* needle is drawn into; success is the displayed interval `[displayed - W_M, displayed + W_M]` intersecting it |
| `W_M`, `m_z`, `ZONE_MARGIN_PM` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | float pm | `W_M` = `WANDER_AMP_PX x 1000 / (needle_len_px x arc_rad)` (21.9 pm at 24 px and 240 deg); `ZONE_MARGIN_PM` 20 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 10 to 50 | derived, Filter | the wander peak in pm (the target never leaves `[W_M, 1000 - W_M]`, so the displayed value plus the wander stays on the dial and the wander is never clamped away) and the margin by which the aim sits inside the zone |
| `DISC_CAP_PM` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | float pm | 400 at defaults | derived: `D_MAX_FRAC_ARC x 1000` | the largest displayed-minus-true the slew may hold: the 40 % fairness cap applied to the slew as well as the target |
| `rate`, `D_eff` | float | D.2 | D.2 | per-lie constants |
| `lie_tau` | float s | D.1 | D.1 | sim-time age since activation |
| `slew_pm` | float pm | [0, 1000] | **presentation-local state**, never persisted, one per ref | the rate-limited value that carries the lie and (D.6) the return |
| `R_act` | float pm/s | 8.33 at defaults | derived | the slew rate bound while ACTIVE; bounded by `GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG`, **not** `GLIDE_RETURN_RATE`; only the `DISC_CAP_PM` clamp may move the slew faster (a documented, bounded exception, OQ-PF-40) |
| `dt` | float s | 0 to `DT_SLEW_MAX_S` 0.1 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | frame delta on `present_time`, clamped | a render hitch cannot make the slew jump |
| `w_pm(t)` | float pm | D.4 | D.4 | wander, added **outside** the slew (its instantaneous speed would otherwise be clipped) |
| `value_lo`, `value_hi` | number | `value_lo` < `value_hi`, in the ref's unit | calibration record | the instrument's range in the ref's own unit (numerals, suit keys) |

**Output Range:** `displayed_pm` is an integer in [0, 1000]. For GLIDE the discrepancy `displayed - true` is `s x delta` (monotone to `D_eff`, then held) plus the slew lag; for FALSE_* the needle departs toward the zone and holds the zone, so the discrepancy is `min(delta, d_aim)`, `d_aim` the aim distance *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. `target_pm` is C0-continuous across both regimes and across a sign flip of `u_aim - u_true` (where the aim distance is 0). **Slew bound and the one exception** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the slew moves at no more than `R_act` except where the `DISC_CAP_PM` clamp binds, which happens only after a truth step larger than the cap minus the current offset or a truth ramp faster than `R_act` held long enough for the lag to reach the cap (5.5 s at a ramp of 100 pm/s from a +100 pm offset, see the example); there the slew is pinned `DISC_CAP_PM` from the truth and follows it. Without the clamp a breach that dropped the true reading by 600 pm would leave the lying needle up to a full dial (a minute at `R_act`) from the room, past the 40 % fairness cap, hiding exactly the emergency `W_MIN_WARNING_S` protects. A FALSE_OK that outlives a degrading truth keeps hiding it (a zone is a place on the dial, not an offset); a GLIDE cannot collapse or grow unseen between Sanity's 5 Hz re-checks (an offset is carried with the truth).

**Restore and slew proof (the law is history-free).** (1) The formula `target_pm` is a pure function of `(lie_tau, u_true, per-lie constants)`. (2) `hard_start` sets `slew_pm := target_pm` (held within `DISC_CAP_PM` of the truth), so on the first frame after a restore or `force_stage` the displayed value equals the formula. (3) The slew is invisible whenever the target's per-frame change is within `R_act x dt`, which is always true for the formula itself (its own drift is at most `rate <= GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG` and `R_act` is that bound). (4) The slew becomes visible only after a **step in the true value** (a Graph step, `apply_damage`) or a **lie hand-off** (a new lie starting while a return is in flight, E-PF-3); it then reconverges in at most `gap / R_act` seconds, at most `0.5 x 1000 / 8.33 = 60 s` for a half-dial step before the cap, and never leaves `DISC_CAP_PM` of the truth (so the worst lag is about `DISC_CAP_PM / R_act` = 48 s; typically a few seconds) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. (5) Therefore the pre-save and post-restore displayed values differ only by the slew gap that was still closing at the save, and the post-restore value is the formula's value from frame one. (6) Both laws re-converge deterministically within one bounded slew because the state is a single clamped follower of a pure target.

**Example:** `arc_deg` 240 (24 deg = 100 pm), kind GLIDE, `rate` 1.19, `D_eff` 24 deg, `s` = +1, `u_true` 700 pm. At `lie_tau` 10 s: `delta = min(11.9, 24) x 1000 / 240 = 49.6 pm`, `target = 749.6`. At 20.2 s `delta = 100 pm`, `target = 800` and holds. At 25 s the true value steps to 650 (a repair): `target = 750` (the 100-pm offset is carried), `R_act = 2.0 x 1000 / 240 = 8.33 pm/s`, so the displayed needle follows down over `50 / 8.33 = 6.0 s` with no snap, and the discrepancy never collapses. FALSE_OK with the zone `[800, 1000]` and `W_M` 21.9 (so `m_z` 21.9 and `u_aim = clamp(u_true, 821.9, 978.1)`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: with `u_true` 650 the aim is 821.9, 171.9 pm away, inside `D_eff` 400 pm: the needle reaches 821.9 and holds, its band `[800, 843.8]` inside the zone; with `u_true` 400 the aim is 421.9 pm away, over the 400 pm cap: the needle stops at 800 and its band `[778.1, 821.9]` only touches the zone edge (a weak lie that Sanity's pre-draw gate should have refused); with `u_true` 350 it stops at 750, the band `[728.1, 771.9]` misses the zone, and `lies_zone_missed` increments. (The earlier example aimed at the zone centre 900 and stopped at 800 for a centre 900 away, which taught players only that violet does not mean wrong.) **Truth ramp, GLIDE offset 100 pm** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the truth rises 100 pm/s; the slew follows at 8.33 pm/s, so displayed-minus-true falls from +100 to -400 pm in `500 / 91.7` = 5.5 s and is then pinned at -400 pm (`DISC_CAP_PM`) while the slew follows the truth at 100 pm/s: bounded by the cap and continuous, a fast move the lie does not choose. **Dial stop:** `u_true` 950, `s` = +1, `delta` 100 pm, `W_M` 21.9: `raw` 1050 clamps to `target` 978.1, so the displayed-minus-true is 28.1 pm (6.7 deg, under `D_VIS_MIN_DEG`: `lie_short_reach`), the gleam, the sound and the full wander run, and a +100 repair that pins the truth at 1000 moves the target to 978.1 and the needle stays on the dial.

### D.4 Wander and the never-rest bound

The wander formula is defined as:

`w_px(t_p) = WANDER_AMP_PX * blend_in(lie_tau) * sin(2 * pi * f_w * t_p + phi_w)`, `blend_in(x) = min(1, x / WANDER_ONSET_S)`, applied along the needle's tangent (a unit vector at angle `phi_n` from the x axis) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

`w_pm = w_px * 1000 / (needle_len_px * arc_deg * pi / 180)`

`f_w = WANDER_F_MIN + (WANDER_F_MAX - WANDER_F_MIN) * (h_wf mod 1001) / 1000`, `phi_w = 2 * pi * (h_wp mod 1000) / 1000`

`dwell_max(A, f, phi_n) = acos(1 - 1 / (A * m)) / (pi * f)`, `m = max(abs(cos phi_n), abs(sin phi_n))` (`A * m >= 1`), worst case `m = 1 / sqrt(2)` at 45 degrees, and the assertion `WANDER_AMP_PX / sqrt(2) >= 1 / (1 - cos(pi * WANDER_F_MIN * NEVER_REST_MAX_S))` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t_p` | float s | >= 0 | `present_time` (PIPE PR-9) | the wander phase carries no information, so it runs on `present_time` |
| `WANDER_AMP_PX` | float art px | 2.2 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 2.1 to 2.6 at `WANDER_F_MIN` 0.40 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Filter | peak excursion of the tip along its tangent; the lower bound is the two-axis assertion above (2.05 at 0.40 Hz) |
| `WANDER_F_MIN`, `WANDER_F_MAX` | float Hz | 0.40 and 0.45 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (min range 0.40 to 0.45); max below 0.5 (ART 7.3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Filter | frequency band; disjoint from the 6 to 10 Hz tremor |
| `WANDER_ONSET_S` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | float s | 1.0 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 0.5 to 3.0 | Filter | the wander fades **in** linearly so the first ACTIVE frame has zero offset (D.1) and the onset rest stays short; also `TREMOR_BLEND_S` (PF-8 `tremor_weight_pm`) |
| `WANDER_BLEND_S` | float s | 3 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 1 to 6 | Filter | the wander fades **out** from the removal frame (D.6), so a return has no step and CLEAR needs a wander of 0 |
| `h_wf`, `h_wp` | uint32 | 0 to 2^32 - 1 | D.14, tags 4 and 5 | per-lie frequency and phase draws |
| `NEVER_REST_MAX_S` | float s | 1.0 (cited ceiling, ART 7.3 and SNV-25; not tunable above 1.0) | art bible | the longest the quantised tip may stay on one pixel |
| `A`, `f`, `phi_n` | float art px, float Hz, deg | `A` 2.2 default; `f` 0.40 to 0.45; `phi_n` 0 to 90 (by symmetry) | `WANDER_AMP_PX`, `f_w`, the needle's pose | amplitude, frequency and tangent angle in the dwell formula *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** `abs(w_px) <= WANDER_AMP_PX`. **Never-rest proof (held state, two axes)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The tip is drawn at an integer art-px position (nearest sampling) and moves along the needle's tangent, so its rounded position is the pair of two rounded axis positions and it changes when either axis's rounded coordinate changes. Per axis the tip is a sinusoid of amplitude `A m_i` (`m_x = abs(cos phi_n)`, `m_y = abs(sin phi_n)`) about an offset `c_i`, the fractional pixel position of the held target. One axis stays on one pixel longest in the unit-wide level band that sits at the sinusoid's peak with its lower edge exactly at the mean, a fraction `acos(1 - 1/(A m_i)) / pi` of the period, so its dwell is `acos(1 - 1/(A m_i)) / (pi f)`. The pair rests only while both axes rest; the worst case aligns both bands on the same peak, and the dwell is then that of the axis with the *larger* amplitude, `m = max(m_x, m_y)`, which is smallest and so worst at 45 degrees (`m = 1/sqrt(2)`). At `A` 2.0 and 0.35 Hz the single-axis dwell is 0.952 s but the 45-degree dwell is **1.158 s**, over the 1.0 s ceiling, and no amplitude inside the old range (1.85 to 2.5) could fix it (it needs 2.59 px at 0.35 Hz); at `A` 2.2 and `WANDER_F_MIN` 0.40 the dwell is **0.959 s at 45 degrees** (0.791 s at 0 and 90 degrees), a margin of 4 %. A brute-force model over a 24 x 24 grid of fractional offsets and needle angles 0 to 90 degrees in 15-degree steps agrees with the analytic bound to 0.02 s (0.942 s measured at 45 degrees; 0.998 s against 1.001 s analytic at `A` 2.0 and 0.405 Hz, the smallest frequency that would have fixed the old amplitude). The proof is for a **single sinusoid**: the two-sine sum (0.6 and 0.4 weights) proposed in a specialist draft has a beat envelope that falls to 0.2 of its peak, in which the rounded tip rests for the length of a beat, so it is not adopted. Under reduced motion the wander is **not scaled** (PF-39), so `RM_GLIDE_SCALE` does not touch the bound. **Onset (measured, bounded):** during ONSET the drift moves the tip at least 0.5 art px per second by `rate_floor` (tip speed `v = needle_len_px x rate x pi / 180`: 0.50 px/s at 24 px and 1.19 deg/s) and the wander fades in linearly over `WANDER_ONSET_S`; the worst-case rest from the first ACTIVE frame, over needle angle, phase and fractional pixel and with no credit for the drift, is **1.15 s** at 0.40 Hz (1.04 s at 0.45 Hz), inside the stated bound `ONSET_REST_MAX_S` = `WANDER_ONSET_S + NEVER_REST_MAX_S` = 2.0 s (AC-PF-46). A lie whose truth sits at a dial stop (`lie_short_reach`) keeps the full wander, because the target clamp leaves the wander margin `W_M` (D.3), and relies on it as its motion mode.

**Example:** `needle_len_px` 24, `arc_deg` 240 (4.19 rad), `WANDER_AMP_PX` 2.2: `w_pm_peak = 2.2 x 1000 / (24 x 4.19) = 21.9 pm` (5.25 deg, 2.2 pixels at the tip). Peak tip speed at 0.45 Hz is `2 pi x 0.45 x 2.2 = 6.22 px/s`, which is 14.9 deg/s angular on a 24-px needle: far above the 0.5 to 2 deg/s band, which is why the band is read as the drift component only (CONFLICT C-1). With hash `H(1, 0x1234, 7, 0)`: `h_wf mod 1001` = 467 gives `f_w = 0.40 + 0.05 x 0.467 = 0.4234 Hz` and `phi_w = 2 pi x 0.105 = 0.66 rad`; the worst-case dwell at 0.4234 Hz is `acos(1 - 1/1.556) / (pi x 0.4234) = 0.906 s` at 45 degrees and 0.746 s at 0 degrees. Assertion at defaults: `2.2 / sqrt(2) = 1.556 >= 1 / (1 - cos(pi x 0.40 x 1.0)) = 1.447` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

### D.5 Leak amplitude (the ACTIVE floor and the discrepancy tail)

The leak amplitude formula (registry: `lie_leak_amplitude`; also D.6 and D.15) is defined as:

`g(d) = clamp((d - LEAK_EPS_DEG) / (LEAK_D_FULL_DEG - LEAK_EPS_DEG), 0, 1)`

ACTIVE *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `a_cand = floor_eff + (AMP_MAX_PM - floor_eff) * g(disc)`, `a_env(t + dt) = max(a_cand(t + dt), a_env(t) - R_AMP_ACT * dt)` (rises are instantaneous, only falls are rate-limited; `R_AMP_ACT = AMP_FLOOR_PM / LEAK_FADE_S` = 266.7 pm/s; `dt` is clamped at `DT_SLEW_MAX_S`; `hard_start: a_env := a_cand`), and on a needle class `amp = a_env`; with the stage-indexed floor `floor_eff = AMP_FLOOR_PM - (AMP_FLOOR_PM - AMP_FLOOR_S3_PM) * r_FLOOR` (user ruling D74; `r_FLOOR` is the D.7 envelope of the effect FLOOR with `GAIN_FLOOR` `[0, 0, 500, 1000]`, so `floor_eff` is 400, 325 and 250 per mille at stages 0 to 1, 2 and 3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

ACTIVE, static-pair classes and numeral-only STALE readouts (BREATH, PF-15; a held digit is treated as a binary surface, `nu` as for a cut frame): `a_cand = floor_eff + (AMP_MAX_PM - floor_eff) * g(nu)` followed by the same `a_env` rule, then `amp = floor_eff + (a_env - floor_eff) * breath01(phi)`, `breath01(phi) = 0.5 - 0.5 * cos(2 * pi * phi)` (D.8; `nu` of D.6; the follower sits on the envelope and never on the breath, whose own falls reach 471 pm/s) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

RETURN (entry removed), **one rule for every `disc_end`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `amp_cand = amp_end * min(1, g(disc) / g(disc_end))` if `g(disc_end) > 0`, else 0; `amp(t + dt) = min(amp(t), AMP_MAX_PM, max(amp_cand, amp(t) - R_AMP * dt))`, `R_AMP = floor_eff(t_rem) / LEAK_FADE_S` latched at removal *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, `amp(0) = amp_end`

CLEAR (PF-12) = the later of `disc < LEAK_EPS_DEG`, `amp = 0` and `t_ret >= WANDER_BLEND_S`

Binary frames: `disc := nu(t)`, the pseudo-discrepancy of D.6 (the afterglow of a static-pair cut uses the same follower)

SOUND level (D.15) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R8)*: `amp_snd = amp` with `breath01 := 1` while ACTIVE (a needle class does not breathe, so `amp_snd = amp`); in RETURN the rule above with `amp_snd_end` in place of `amp_end`; the sound is modulated once, by the envelope Audio runs from the ON request

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `disc` | float deg | 0 to `D_eff` | `abs(slew_pm - u_true) x arc_deg / 1000` (D.3 slew value, wander excluded) | displayed-minus-true discrepancy |
| `LEAK_EPS_DEG` | float deg | 0.25 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 0.1 to 0.5 | Filter | below this the lie is visually closed (CLEAR) |
| `LEAK_D_FULL_DEG` | float deg | 20 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 10 to 40 | Filter | discrepancy at which the leak is full |
| `AMP_FLOOR_PM` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | int pm | 400 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 250 to 600 (PF-15) | Filter | the stage 0 to 1 gleam floor: leak while ACTIVE even at zero discrepancy; also the largest floor, so the worst case of the RETURN flash coupling |
| `AMP_FLOOR_S3_PM` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | int pm | 250 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 250 to `AMP_FLOOR_PM` | Filter | the stage-3 gleam floor (user ruling D74, derived below) |
| `floor_eff`, `r_FLOOR` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | int pm, float | `floor_eff` in `[AMP_FLOOR_S3_PM, AMP_FLOOR_PM]`; `r_FLOOR` in [0, 1] | presentation-local, D.7 | the floor in force and its envelope (`hard_start` sets it to its target; never saved) |
| `AMP_MAX_PM` | int pm | 1000 | Filter | full amplitude; scales the gleam alpha and the sound gain; the gleam luminance ceiling is 0.9 x `GLOW_HDR_THRESHOLD` (cited, Lighting) and the peak channel is `LEAK_MAX_CH` |
| `amp_end`, `disc_end` | int pm, float deg | `amp_end` 0 to 1000; `disc_end` 0 to `D_eff` | captured on the run the entry left `sanity.perception` | values at removal (presentation-local) |
| `t_ret` | float s | >= 0 | `present_time` since removal | seconds since removal on `present_time` |
| `LEAK_FADE_S` | float s | 1.5 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 1 to 4 | Filter | sets `R_AMP`: the time the amplitude floor takes to fade when a lie ends (the registry value is unchanged); coupled to `AMP_FLOOR_PM` by `AMP_FLOOR_PM / LEAK_FADE_S <= 343` (D.6) |
| `R_AMP` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | float pm/s | 266.7 at stages 0 to 1, 216.7 at stage 2, 166.7 at stage 3 | derived: `floor_eff(t_rem) / LEAK_FADE_S`, latched at removal | the fastest the amplitude may fall in RETURN; keeps the afterglow a single-direction change of `Y_v x R_AMP / 1000` per second (0.0776 at stage 1, the worst case; 0.0485 at stage 3), under the 0.10 pair threshold, and keeps the time the floor takes to fade at `LEAK_FADE_S` at every stage |
| `a_cand`, `a_env` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | int pm | `floor_eff` to `AMP_MAX_PM` | derived, presentation-local | the discrepancy-driven candidate amplitude and the rate-limited envelope that carries it; `a_env` is also the SOUND amplitude `amp_snd` (D.15) |
| `R_AMP_ACT` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | float pm/s | 266.7 at defaults | derived: `AMP_FLOOR_PM / LEAK_FADE_S` | the fastest an ACTIVE amplitude may fall; the same bound as the largest `R_AMP`, so assertion (m) covers ACTIVE and RETURN alike |

**Output Range:** ACTIVE `amp` is in `[floor_eff, AMP_MAX_PM]` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, so the leak is never below the floor in force while ACTIVE, including the first frame (there is no onset hole: the pure-discrepancy form would be 0 at `lie_tau` 0). **ACTIVE falls are rate-limited** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: a truth step toward a GLIDE needle collapses `disc` by up to `DISC_CAP_PM` (96 deg) in one frame, which would have dropped `amp` by 600 to 750 pm at once (0.175 to 0.218 of relative luminance, above the 0.10 pair threshold as a single step, and a tell that the truth had just moved); `a_env` now falls at no more than 266.7 pm/s (0.0776 of relative luminance per second), so the gleam fades over about 2.3 s and the step is invisible as an event (example: `disc` 96 deg to 0 at once, `amp` 1000 to the floor 400 in `600 / 266.7` = 2.25 s). Rises stay instantaneous because they follow the glide, whose slope is at most `600 / 19.75 x 2.0` = 61 pm/s. The only discontinuity left in a gleam amplitude is the **rise-only onset step** (0 to `floor_eff` on the first ACTIVE frame, SNV-13 full leak from frame one), which is a single-direction change followed by no fall for at least half a breath period (D.8, PF-32). RETURN `amp` is in `[0, amp_end]` and **non-increasing whatever the truth does** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the follower is `min`-ed with its own previous value, so a truth that moves away during the return holds the amplitude at `amp_end` and never raises it (the earlier ratio could reach 1409 pm); it falls no faster than `R_AMP` (266.7 pm/s at stage 1, 166.7 at stage 3) and no faster than the discrepancy tail. The earlier two branches switched at `disc_end = 2 x LEAK_EPS_DEG`, so 0.49 deg and 0.51 deg removals produced an 83 ms drop and a 1.5 s fade; **one rule** now gives them series within 0.7 pm. **Reaching zero:** the amplitude is 0 at CLEAR (PF-12), not at `LEAK_EPS_DEG`: when `disc` is already under `LEAK_EPS_DEG` at removal the amplitude still fades at `R_AMP` (1.5 s from 400) and the record stays RETURN, with `lie_id` held, until the wander has faded too (the earlier text left RETURN while the violet fade and the sound were still running, which put a gleam on an honest record and made the two ACs contradict). For large `disc_end` the discrepancy tail is the slower term and the series is unchanged (hold at 1000 until 9.3 s, 0 at 15.9 s for a 48-deg removal). **Continuity at removal:** at `t_ret` 0 `amp = amp_end` whatever the end type; EXPIRED, INVALIDATED and DEBUNKED share the formula and therefore the series (AC-PF-16). The sound level follows `amp` (D.15).

**Example (stage 1, floor 400; the stage-3 case is in the derivation below)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: onset: `lie_tau` 0, `disc` 0 gives `amp = 400` (the gleam is visible and the sound request is on at ACTIVE frame 1). At `disc` 10 deg: `g = 9.75 / 19.75 = 0.494`, `amp = 400 + 600 x 0.494 = 696`. At 20 deg: 1000. End after a full glide, `disc_end` 48 deg, `amp_end` 1000, `GLIDE_RETURN_RATE` 3: the leak holds at 1000 until `disc` falls below 20 deg (9.3 s after removal), then falls linearly to 0 at `disc` 0.25 deg (15.9 s after removal). End at `disc_end` 10 deg (`amp_end` 696, `g(disc_end)` 0.494): `amp = 696 x g(disc) / 0.494` is 339 at 5 deg (1.67 s), 54 at 1 deg (3.0 s) and 0 at 0.25 deg (3.25 s); the discrepancy tail (214 pm/s) is slower than `R_AMP`, so it governs. End at `disc_end` 0.1 deg (a debunk at `lie_tau` 0.1 s) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `amp_end` 400 falls at 266.7 pm/s to 0 in exactly 1.5 s; the record stays RETURN until CLEAR at `WANDER_BLEND_S` 3.0 s. Boundary pair `disc_end` 0.49 and 0.51 deg: `amp_end` 407.3 and 407.9, both fall at 266.7 pm/s and reach 0 at 1.53 s. A `disc_end` of 4.75 deg (`amp_end` 537) is the steepest case: the amplitude falls at the full `R_AMP`, 266.7 pm/s, and reaches 0 after 2.01 s, a single-direction change of `0.291 x 266.7 / 1000 = 0.0776` per second (a scan over `disc_end` from 0.1 to 100 deg finds no steeper fall). Truth moving away: `disc_end` 48 deg and the truth rising 4 deg/s against the 3 deg/s follower, so the discrepancy grows: `amp` stays at 1000 and never exceeds it.

**Stage-indexed gleam floor (USER RULING, design-review decision D74, OQ-PF-37)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. *Ruling.* Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): lever (a), `AMP_FLOOR_PM` falls toward 250 with stage. It is driven by the stage, not by lie age: the stage is saved SIM state, so a restore reproduces the floor, while the envelope `r_FLOOR` is presentation-local and starts steady on `hard_start`, exactly like the rim envelope; lie identity, kind, modes and trajectory do not depend on the stage (PF-21 "lies are not ramped by stage" still holds, only the floor of the gleam amplitude follows the viewer's stage envelope). `MODE_DROP_PM` stays 0.

*Derivation.* (1) Stage 0 to 1 keep 400: the SN-15 L-L and SNV-13 onset-hole argument is tightest on the first lie and stage 1 lies are needles and readouts only. (2) The stage-3 endpoint is the lower bound of the registered range, `AMP_FLOOR_S3_PM` = 250: the ruling says "toward 250" and the range is not re-opened. (3) Stage 2 is the midpoint so that each stage step is equal, 75 per mille: `floor(2) = 400 - 75 = 325`, hence `GAIN_FLOOR[2] = (400 - 325) / (400 - 250) x 1000 = 500` and `GAIN_FLOOR[3] = 1000`. Constraints the values satisfy: (C1) `250 <= floor_eff <= 400` and non-increasing in stage; (C2) the gleam is never off (the floor is above 0 and the onset hole stays closed); (C3) flash safety *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: every amplitude fall, ACTIVE or RETURN, is bounded by `R_AMP` (the D.6 coupling at the lowest floor, 0.0332, is withdrawn as redundant with that bound), the RETURN fall `Y_v x floor_eff / LEAK_FADE_S / 1000` is largest at stage 1 (0.0776, unchanged) and 0.0485 at stage 3, the stage-3 breath depth 0.75 and the floor's own slope `(400 - 250) / 1000 / 8 = 0.01875` per second pass D.11 (patch bound 0.0738, region 0.0201); (C4) sound *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R8)*: the D.15 onset request is the raw floor itself, 400, 325 and 250 per mille at stages 0 to 1, 2 and 3 (Audio converts; the lone-lie trough at 250 stays above `HAL_ACTIVE_MIN_DB`, D.15); (C5) visibility: `250` is `[PLACEHOLDER — verify at the stage-3 arm of the AC-PF-29a hue-removed protocol and the Lighting spike]`, and it sits below `DECOY_LEAK_ALPHA_PM` 300 (routed to Lighting).

*What the lever does and does not do (re-review, consequence of D74, not a re-opening)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. On a binary surface `g(nu)` saturates at 1 (the pseudo-discrepancy is the full `LEAK_D_FULL_DEG` from the cut), so the **peak violet is 1000 at every stage**; the stage-indexed floor lowers only the trough of the breath and the amplitude at zero discrepancy. The lever therefore changes the **trough-to-peak dwell**, the share of a breath cycle spent below half amplitude (500 pm): `dwell(stage) = acos(1 - 2 x (500 - floor_eff) / (1000 - floor_eff)) / pi` = 0.268 at floors 400 (stages 0 to 1), 0.341 at 325 (stage 2) and 0.392 at 250 (stage 3), at depths 0.6, 0.675 and 0.75. That is a modest move (about 12 points of a cycle between stage 1 and stage 3), and V7 and the stage-3 arm of AC-PF-29a now measure it: V7 is read against the dwell at the stage where the lie was seen, and AC-PF-29a samples each trial at a recorded breath phase and reports the scores for trials below and above 500 pm separately. Levers (b) `MODE_DROP_PM` and (c) of OQ-PF-37 stay specified and ready if V7 still fails.

*Worked example, stage 3 steady (`floor_eff` 250).* Onset: `lie_tau` 0, `disc` 0 gives `amp = 250`. At `disc` 10 deg: `g = 9.75 / 19.75 = 0.4937`, `amp = 250 + 750 x 0.4937 = 620.3` (stage 1: 696.2). At 20 deg: 1000. A static-pair gleam breathes between 250 and 1000 (depth 0.75). A debunk at `lie_tau` 0.1 s: `amp_end` 250, `R_AMP = 250 / 1.5 = 166.7` pm/s, `amp` reaches 0 in exactly 1.5 s, a single-direction change of `0.291 x 166.7 / 1000 = 0.0485` per second. A direct `force_stage` 1 to 3: `floor_eff` moves 400 to 250 at 18.75 pm/s over 8 s, a luminance change of `0.291 x 18.75 / 1000 = 0.0055` per second. The steepest-fall case above (`disc_end` 4.75 deg, 266.7 pm/s) is a stage 0 to 1 case; it bounds every stage.

### D.6 Return path (the glide-return)

The return path formula is defined as:

`slew_pm(t + dt) = clamp(slew_pm(t) + clamp(u_true(t) - slew_pm(t), -R_ret * dt, +R_ret * dt), u_true(t) - DISC_CAP_PM, u_true(t) + DISC_CAP_PM)`, `R_ret = rm_ret * GLIDE_RETURN_RATE * 1000 / arc_deg`, `rm_ret = max(rm, RM_RETURN_FLOOR)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (the `DISC_CAP_PM` clamp of D.3, applied after the rate limit, so a truth moving away during RETURN cannot widen the gap past 400 pm)

`t_return = disc_end / (rm_ret * GLIDE_RETURN_RATE)` for a static true value; `t_clear = max(t_return, amp_end / R_AMP, WANDER_BLEND_S)` (the time to CLEAR; published on the dev channel as `t_return_max`, OQ-PF-39)

`w_ret_pm = w_pm_end_shape * (1 - smoothstep(0, WANDER_BLEND_S, t_ret))` (the wander fades out with the return so the removal has no step)

`nu(t) = max(0, nu_end - rm_ret * GLIDE_RETURN_RATE * t_ret)` (pseudo-discrepancy of a binary frame after its cut-back); while ACTIVE, `nu(lie_tau) = LEAK_D_FULL_DEG * smoothstep(0, SWAP_LEAD_S, lie_tau)` (the gleam rises to full at the cut, then holds); `nu_end := nu(lie_tau at removal)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (`LEAK_D_FULL_DEG` when the removal is at or after the cut, `lie_tau >= SWAP_LEAD_S`; less before it). The frame cut itself happens at `lie_tau = SWAP_LEAD_S`: a removal inside the lead shows the honest frame throughout and only the gleam runs its RETURN from the in-flight `nu`, so RETURN starts from the in-flight discrepancy for every end type and a false frame is never shown without its leak

Build assertions: `rm_ret * GLIDE_RETURN_RATE >= rm * GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG` for every `rm` (3.0 >= 2.0 at `rm` 1.0; 2.5 >= 1.4 at 0.7), and `Y_v * AMP_FLOOR_PM / LEAK_FADE_S / 1000 < FLASH_LUM_DELTA` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (0.0776 < 0.10: the fastest single-direction fall of any gleam amplitude, ACTIVE or RETURN, D.5; `AMP_FLOOR_PM` 600 with `LEAK_FADE_S` 1.0 fails it). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The earlier third assertion, `Y_v * (AMP_MAX_PM - AMP_FLOOR_S3_PM) / 1000 / (LEAK_D_FULL_DEG - LEAK_EPS_DEG) * GLIDE_RETURN_RATE < FLASH_LUM_DELTA` (0.0332, knob ceiling 9.05), is withdrawn: it was derived from the ACTIVE form, not from the unified RETURN rule (whose slope is `amp_end / g(disc_end)` per unit `g`, up to 1000, so the true ceiling would be about 6.79) and it is redundant, because the `R_AMP` clamp already bounds every fall at 0.0776 whatever `GLIDE_RETURN_RATE` is.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GLIDE_RETURN_RATE` | float deg/s | 3.0 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 2.0 to 8.0; registered | Filter | return speed |
| `rm`, `rm_ret` | float | `rm` 1.0 or `RM_GLIDE_SCALE` 0.6; `rm_ret = max(rm, RM_RETURN_FLOOR)`, `RM_RETURN_FLOOR` 0.83 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (range 0.8 to 1.0) | PF-39 | the glide scales by `rm`; the return by `rm_ret`, which caps the reduced-motion return at about 1.2 times the normal one *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `disc_end` | float deg | 0 to `D_eff` | captured at removal | discrepancy when the entry left perception |
| `w_pm_end_shape` | float pm | D.4 | the wander term at removal, continuing its phase | continuity |
| `nu_end` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | float deg | 0 to `LEAK_D_FULL_DEG` (20) | derived: `nu(lie_tau at removal)` | the pseudo-discrepancy in flight at removal: 20 for a removal at or after the cut, and the smoothstep value for a removal inside the `SWAP_LEAD_S` lead (5.19 deg at `lie_tau` 1.0 s) |
| `FLASH_LUM_DELTA` | float | 0.10 (cited, PIPE PR-13's 10 %) | Pipeline | the luminance-change threshold |
| `Y_v` | float | 0.291 | computed from `#B07BE6` | relative luminance of the violet (linear 0.2126 R + 0.7152 G + 0.0722 B) |

**Output Range:** the follower is monotone toward the live true value and cannot overshoot, settle or snap. It tracks a moving true value at the same bounded rate. **Over-rate exception** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the `DISC_CAP_PM` clamp (the same clamp as ACTIVE, D.3) holds the follower within 400 pm of the truth, so when the truth moves away faster than `R_ret` the needle is pinned 400 pm from it and follows at the truth's own speed, which can exceed `GLIDE_RETURN_RATE`; this is the same bounded exception as `R_act` (OQ-PF-40), and the amplitude is unaffected (it only falls). `t_return` is at most `D_MAX_FRAC_ARC x arc_deg / GLIDE_RETURN_RATE = 96 / 3 = 32 s` at defaults (38.4 s at `rm` 0.7, since `rm_ret` is 0.83, against 53.3 s before the cap *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*). CLEAR comes at `t_clear`, never earlier than `WANDER_BLEND_S` 3.0 s and never before the amplitude is 0. The same follower carries the lie hand-off: a new lie on a ref in `RETURN` starts from its own formula and the ACTIVE slew (D.3) closes the gap from the in-flight value at `GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG`. **Binary frames** (a static-pair SPRITE_SWAP, PF-19) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: a removal at or after the cut cuts the frame back at removal and the gleam decays on `nu(t)` through D.5's `g(nu)` on the `GLIDE_RETURN_RATE` schedule, `(nu_end - LEAK_EPS_DEG) / GLIDE_RETURN_RATE = 19.75 / 3 = 6.6 s`; a removal inside the lead (`lie_tau < SWAP_LEAD_S`) never shows the false frame, and the gleam decays from the in-flight `nu_end` (vector: the vector lie `H(1, 0x1234, 7, 0)`, first breath period 5828 ms, removal at `lie_tau` 1.0 s, breath phase 0.172 so `breath01` 0.263, `nu_end` = `20 x smoothstep(1/3)` = 5.19 deg, the envelope `a_env` = `amp_snd_end` 550 and the gleam `amp_end` = `400 + (550 - 400) x 0.263` = 439 at stage 1; `nu` reaches `LEAK_EPS_DEG` at `(5.19 - 0.25) / 3` = 1.65 s, the gleam `amp` reaches 0 at `439 / 266.7` = 1.65 s, the sound `amp_snd` at `550 / 266.7` = 2.06 s, CLEAR at `WANDER_BLEND_S` 3.0 s). **Continuous frames** (the rotor, D.17) wind their cadence down at a constant rate and snap at the minimum cadence.

**Example:** a 60-deg discrepancy at removal: 30 s at 2.0 deg/s, **20 s at 3.0**, 10 s at 6.0, and 24.0 s under reduced motion (`rm_ret` 0.83 gives 2.5 deg/s; it was 33.3 s at 1.8 deg/s) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. A live 60-deg lie at 2.0 deg/s took 30 s to build, which is why the lower limit is `GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG`: below it a debunked needle would outlast a live lie, and a DEBUNKED would be a punishment. Flash coupling *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the ACTIVE-form coupling (0.0332 at the stage-3 floor) is withdrawn, so `GLIDE_RETURN_RATE` and `LEAK_D_FULL_DEG` are no longer coupled by a flash ceiling (the `R_AMP` clamp bounds every fall; at rate 8 and `LEAK_D_FULL_DEG` 10 the unclamped slope would be 0.239 per second (1000 x 8 / 9.75 = 820 pm/s), and the clamp holds it at 0.0776). The amplitude-floor coupling: `0.291 x 400 / 1.5 / 1000 = 0.0776 < 0.10` (stage 0 to 1, the worst case; stage 3 is 0.0485). Binary frame: a cracked-housing frame cuts back at removal; the gleam, at 1000, decays to 0 over 6.6 s.

### D.7 Stage ramp envelope

The stage ramp formula is defined as:

`target_e = gate_e * GAIN_e[stage] / 1000`, with `gate_e = 1` for FLOOR and OUTLINE, `gate_RIM = 1` while a reach lie is ACTIVE for the viewer and 0 otherwise in LIE_BOUND mode (the shipped default; `gate_RIM = 1` in the opt-in AMBIENT mode), and `gate_BLEED = 1` only when `AMBIENT_FRINGES_ENABLED` is on (0 at the shipped default) (PF-23, PF-24, OQ-PF-1, OQ-PF-2) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

`r_e(t + dt) = r_e(t) + clamp(target_e - r_e(t), -rate_s * dt, +rate_s * dt)` with `rate_s = abs(target_e - r_e(t_s)) / STAGE_RAMP_S` latched at each target change `t_s`, on `present_time`, `hard_start: r_e := target_e`

`effective_amplitude_e = r_e * amplitude_e`

Build assertion: `STAGE_RAMP_S >= max(ramp_min(D.11), BREATH_MAX_MS / 1000 + RAMP_BREATH_MARGIN_S)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `e` | enum | RIM, BLEED, FLOOR (and OUTLINE with `TELL_RAMP_S`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | PF-22, PF-28, D.5 | the stage-bound effect |
| `GAIN_e[stage]` | int pm | RIM `[0, 0, 600, 1000]`, BLEED `[0, 0, 0, 1000]` (`RIM_STAGE_GAIN_PM[2]`, `[3]`, `BLEED_STAGE_GAIN_PM[3]`, `[PLACEHOLDER]`), FLOOR `[0, 0, 500, 1000]` (`FLOOR_STAGE_GAIN_PM`, derived in D.5 as `(AMP_FLOOR_PM - floor(stage)) / (AMP_FLOOR_PM - AMP_FLOOR_S3_PM)`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | Filter | the envelope target per stage; `stage` is `sanity_view.stage`, which steps instantly at `STAGE_ENTER` 70000 / 55000 / 25000 with `STAGE_HYST` 2000 and `STAGE_DWELL_RUNS` 25 (cited from Sanity's registry entry; the stage-2 entry was 45000 before X11/D64 raised it to 55000, and the Filter reads whatever Sanity registers) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* |
| `r_e` | float | [0, 1] | presentation-local | the envelope |
| `STAGE_RAMP_S` | float s | 8 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 6.5 to 20; registered | Filter | seconds for every envelope transition (a stage step or a reversal, whatever its size) |
| `TELL_RAMP_S` | float s | 8 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 6.5 to 15 | Filter | the same form for the class-(c) outline (VS-B) |
| `BREATH_MAX_MS` | int ms | 6000 (named here, value from ART 5.5 / HR-7) | art bible | the breath period upper bound |
| `RAMP_BREATH_MARGIN_S` | float s | 0.5 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Filter | margin so the onset cannot read as one more breath |
| `dt` | float s | frame delta on `present_time`; 0 while PAUSED | PIPE PR-9 | the envelope's frame step |
| `t_s` | float s | >= 0 | `present_time` | the frame on which `target_e` last changed (a stage step or a reversal) |
| `rate_s` | float per s | 0 to `1 / STAGE_RAMP_S` | latched at `t_s` | the slew rate of the current transition, so that every transition takes `STAGE_RAMP_S` |

**Output Range:** `r_e` is in [0, 1], slope at most `1 / STAGE_RAMP_S` = 0.125 per second, monotone between reversals, with no overshoot. Every transition, a reversal included, takes exactly `STAGE_RAMP_S`: the rate is re-latched from the current value, so a reversal from 0.5 returns to 0 in 8 s at 0.0625 per second. Because every transition (not only a full 0-to-1 swing) lasts `STAGE_RAMP_S`, the build assertion protects every MVP transition, including the 0.6 stage-2 step and the 0.6-to-1.0 stage-3 step. **Floor:** `STAGE_RAMP_S` and `TELL_RAMP_S` are held at or above `max(3.91 s (D.11 patch-level ramp_min), 6.0 + 0.5 s) = 6.5 s` by a build assertion; the breath rule governs, the flash model does not bind (D.11). The ramp's steepest slope (0.125 per second) is below the breath's steepest slope (`BREATH_DEPTH x pi / T_min = 0.47` per second), so a ramp reads as drift, not as a breath.

**Example:** stage 1 to 2 at `t` 0, `STAGE_RAMP_S` 8: `r_RIM` rises at a latched 0.6 / 8 = 0.075 per second to its 0.6 target in **8.0 s**, longer than the 6 s breath upper bound. At 5 s (the earliest reversal, `STAGE_DWELL_RUNS` 25) the stage falls back to 1: `r_RIM` is 0.375, the rate re-latches to 0.375 / 8 = 0.047 per second, and it falls to 0 over the next 8.0 s, never restarting at 0. Stage 2 to 3: the target rises from 0.6 to 1.0 at 0.05 per second, in 8.0 s. `force_stage` 3: `r_RIM` is 1.0 on the first frame. The floor envelope runs the same form *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: stage 1 to 2 moves `r_FLOOR` from 0 to 0.5 in 8.0 s (`floor_eff` 400 to 325 at 9.4 pm/s) and stage 2 to 3 moves it 0.5 to 1.0 (325 to 250); `force_stage` 3 sets `floor_eff` 250 on the first frame; the RIM entry above assumes a reach lie is ACTIVE (LIE_BOUND), otherwise `target_RIM` is 0.

### D.8 Breath (per-cycle period, floor and harmonic dead zone)

The breath formula is defined as:

`T_n_ms = BREATH_MIN_MS + (h_cycle(n) mod 2001)`, then `T_n_ms = deadzone_push(T_n_ms, {2 * S_i, 3 * S_i : i in PERIODIC_SOURCES}, BREATH_DEADZONE_FRAC)`

`phi_n(t + dt) = phi_n(t) + dt / (T_n_ms / 1000)`; at `phi >= 1`: `phi -= 1`, `n += 1`

`amp_b = amp * (BREATH_FLOOR_PM + (1000 - BREATH_FLOOR_PM) * (0.5 - 0.5 * cos(2 * pi * phi))) / 1000`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BREATH_MIN_MS`, `BREATH_MAX_MS` | int ms | 4000, 6000 (named here, values from ART 5.5 / HR-7) | art bible | the period band |
| `h_cycle(n)` | uint32 | 0 to 2^32 - 1 | D.14 (tag 1 plus the cycle index) | per-cycle draw of `H(viewer, ref_key, lie_key, epoch)`: a lie's gleam breath uses its own `(ref_key, lie_key)` with `epoch` 0; the rim, bleed and outline use the reserved effect ids and the envelope's `epoch` (D.14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `S_i` | float ms | the alarm sweep `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` x 1000 / `TICK_RATE` (registered single alarm rhythm, Emitter State: 128 ticks = 2133 ms, range 120 to 180 = 2000 to 3000 ms; Audio's duty `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` 250, leading edge is the contract, Audio CX-A20, F6; OQ-PF-13 closed); `tell_beat` period (Attention-owned) | owners | periodic sources to avoid locking to |
| `BREATH_DEADZONE_FRAC` | float | 0.06 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 0 to 0.10 | Filter | half-width of the dead zone as a fraction of `k x S_i`; active for every registered `S_i` (the alarm sweep is) |
| `BREATH_LOCK_CEIL` | float | 0.30 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 0.2 to 0.5 (ratification OQ-PF-24) | Filter / systems-designer | peak normalised cross-correlation a breath may show against a periodic source (AC-PF-27) |
| `deadzone_push` | function | int ms in, int ms in [`BREATH_MIN_MS`, `BREATH_MAX_MS`] out | Filter (D.8) | redraw with the cycle index bumped up to 3 times, else clamp to the nearer zone boundary inside the band (deterministic) |
| `BREATH_FLOOR_PM` | int pm | 400 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 250 to 700 (PF-30) | Filter | the trough, never 0 (a fringe that blinks out is unleaked) |
| `amp` | int pm | 0 to 1000 | the fringe's stage envelope (D.7) or the outline envelope | the amplitude being modulated |
| `phi` | float | [0, 1) | `present_time` | cycle phase; the cosine minimum is at the wrap, so a period change never kinks |

**Output Range:** `T_n` is in [4000, 6000] ms minus any dead zone; `amp_b` is in `[amp x BREATH_FLOOR_PM / 1000, amp]`. **The harmonic:** every legal period equals `2S` for some sweep period `S` in 2 to 3 s (and `3S` at the 6 s edge when `S` is 2 s), so a fixed period locks 2:1 against a fixed sweep; SNV-26 forbids locking. Per-cycle jitter makes an accidental alignment last about one cycle in expectation, and the dead zone closes the rest. With `S` 2.5 s the zone is `5000 x (1 +- 0.06)` = [4700, 5300] ms (30 % of the band); with `S` 2.0 s the zones are [3760, 4240] and [5640, 6360], leaving [4240, 5640]; at the registered `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` 128 (`S` 2133 ms) the zone around `2S` is [4011, 4523] ms and `3S` (6400 ms, zone [6016, 6784]) lies outside the band.

**Example:** `H(1, 0x1234, 7, 0)` (D.14), cycle 0: `h_cycle(0) mod 2001` = 1828, so `T_0 = 5828 ms`; cycle 1: 1740 gives `T_1 = 5740 ms`; cycle 2: `T_2 = 5735 ms`. At `phi` 0.5 `amp_b = amp`; at `phi` 0 `amp_b = 0.4 amp`. Two simultaneous fringes use different `(subject, seat)` keys and therefore different sequences.

**The lie gleam breath and the sound envelope** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the same per-cycle draw drives the static-pair gleam breath (PF-15 table): D.5's `breath01(phi)` swings the gleam between `floor_eff` and `AMP_MAX_PM` (depth 0.6 at stages 0 to 1, the same `d_b` as the fringe; 0.75 at stage 3, `d_g` of D.11, re-checked there) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, keyed on the lie's `(viewer, ref_key, lie_key, 0)` and dead-zoned like the fringe; the lie's own cycle 0 starts at its trough (`phi := 0` on the first ACTIVE frame and at `hard_start`, a presentation-local phase), so the onset step of D.5 is followed by a rise, never a fall, for at least half a period (2 s) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The sound envelope of PF-20 draws one period per lie from tag 11, `T_s = 4000 + (sub(H, 11) mod 2001)` ms, dead-zoned, trough `SOUND_BREATH_FLOOR_PM`: 5473 ms for `H(1, 0x1234, 7, 0)`.

### D.9 Lying-digit tick schedule

The lying-digit tick formula is defined as:

`T_tick = max(LIE_TICK_MS, N_r * 334)`

`t_k = k * T_tick + j * T_tick / N_r` on `present_time`, `j` the readout's stagger index (ordered by hash tag 6); the stagger spacing `T_tick / N_r` is never below 334 ms, the single-readout floor

`q_k = clamp(round(slew_value(t_k) / lsb), q_lo, q_hi)`, `q_lo = round(value_lo / lsb)`, `q_hi = round(value_hi / lsb)`; in TICK mode, if `q_k == q_{k-1}`, `q_k = q_{k-1} + sgn_k` (one `lsb`, sign from hash tag 6, flipped if it would leave `[q_lo, q_hi]`), so the digit changes every tick; in STALE mode `q_k` follows the **displayed** needle value by ticks while the lie is in ONSET and is **held from arrival** (when `delta_pm` reaches its aim), never the zone centre while the needle is still short of it, so the digit does not change while the ship changes. During RETURN the numeral keeps the TICK rule on the returning `slew_value` and switches to the honest grid at CLEAR (PF-18). A region gap governor defers any lying tick due less than 334 ms after the previous lying tick in the region; it never defers an honest swap, and the lying-to-honest switch lands on the next HUD grid boundary (PF-18) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `LIE_TICK_MS` | int ms | 400 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 334 to 1000; registered | Filter | single-readout interval. The **floor is 334 ms**: 333 ms is 3.003 transitions per second against AC-SN-149 "no more than 3 per second" |
| `N_r` | int | 1 to `LIES_MAX` (4, cited, Sanity) | derived | lying readouts in the same ninth of the displayed picture, counting HUD-layer readouts |
| `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` | int | 3 (cited, Pipeline) | registry | transition ceiling |
| `lsb` | number | > 0 | calibration record (PF-45) | the readout's least-significant unit (one display unit) |
| `q_lo`, `q_hi` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | int | `round(value_lo / lsb)`, `round(value_hi / lsb)` | calibration record (PF-45) | the numeral's valid range in `lsb` steps; every TICK and STALE value is clamped to it, and the TICK step flips sign rather than leave it |
| `mode` | enum | TICK (kind GLIDE), STALE (kinds FALSE_OK, FALSE_FAIL) | PF-18 | the two lying-readout modes |

**Output Range:** `T_tick` in {400, 668, 1002, 1336} ms for `N_r` 1 to 4 at the default, with stagger spacing 400, 334, 334 and 334 ms; because consecutive transitions in a region are at least 334 ms apart, no closed 1 s window holds more than 3 (the reasoning that set the 334 ms single-readout floor), and the mean rate `N_r / T_tick` is 2.50, 2.994, 2.994 and 2.994 per second. Without the scheduler four readouts at 400 ms would make 10 transitions per second, 3.3 times the ceiling. At `N_r` 4 the continuous cadence falls to 0.75 Hz: still never stopped, still distinguishable from an honest numeral (which changes on the HUD-owned `HONEST_TICK_MS` hold grid), but weaker (risk in Open Questions). Reduced motion never lowers `LIE_TICK_MS` (it may be raised to 600). When `N_r` changes mid-run (a lie starts or ends) `T_tick` is re-evaluated at the next tick and the gap governor still guarantees 334 ms between lying ticks across the change, and the lying-to-honest switch at CLEAR lands on the next HUD grid boundary, where the HUD arbiter (not the governor) admits or skips it (AC-PF-75b, AC-PF-75c) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

**Example:** three lying readouts in one region: `T_tick = max(400, 3 x 334) = 1002 ms` each, phases 0, 334 and 668 ms, so transitions fall every 334 ms: a closed 1 s window from 0 holds 0, 334 and 668 ms (the next is at 1002 ms), never four.

**Flash counting unit and the honest numerals (USER RULING X9/D91, OQ-PF-12)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): a flash event is one opposing pair of luminance transitions, so the counting unit is `flash_events = ceil(n_transitions / 2)` over a rolling second (the registry's `flash_count`), and `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` is weighted per area with the creative-director exemptions (sub-threshold digit swaps and prompt show-starts exempt; the strict ceiling kept for the Alarm Red mark and under reduce-flashing, which defaults ON at first launch, X10). Filter side: D.9 does **not** use the headroom the unit allows. The scheduler keeps the strict transition form (`N_r / T_tick <= 3` transitions per second, 334 ms gap) because Sanity's AC-SN-149 ("no more than 3 changes per second") is a transition cap, a separate rule from the flash unit, and because the strict ceiling is the one that binds under the default reduce-flashing setting. Honest numerals on the ratified `HONEST_TICK_MS` 500 ms grid are sub-threshold digit swaps and, in WEIGHTED mode, are exempt, so they take no slot in `N_r` (in STRICT, the reduce-flashing default, they are counted and the HUD arbiter skips an honest swap the window cannot hold: PF-18, AC-PF-75c) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; even counted, the worst case is 2 transitions per second per numeral, and a region holding the Filter's 3 lying transitions plus one honest numeral's 2 is `ceil((3 + 2) / 2) = 3 <= FLASH_MAX_PER_S`.

### D.10 Fringe width at ladder step k

The fringe width formula is defined as:

`W_screen(n, k) = n * k` screen px for a mark of `n` art px

`n = RIM_FRINGE_PX` (rim band), `n = 2` (crewmate outline, fixed by ART 5.5, not a knob), `n = GLEAM_EDGE_PX` (gleam), `k in [1, k_auto + 2]`, `k_auto = floor(min(W / 640, H / 360))`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `n` | int art px | 1 to 3 | `RIM_FRINGE_PX` 2 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (1 to 3), outline 2 (fixed), `GLEAM_EDGE_PX` `[PLACEHOLDER]` (1 to 2; 2 at `k` 1, else 1, per Instruments' `gleam_edge_px`) | mark width in the 640x360 grid |
| `k` | int | 1 to 8 | PIPE PR-1, C6 (cited) | integer zoom step |
| `W`, `H` | int px | display size | OS | the window's pixel size |

**Output Range:** `W_screen` is always an exact integer multiple of `k` (no sub-art-pixel softness), from 1 (rim 1 at `k` 1; the gleam is never below 2) to 24 (rim 3 at `k` 8). Under render pass B the leak material takes `k` as a uniform and does no arithmetic on it. A zoom change is a cut (PIPE C6): widths are re-derived on the first frame of the new rung and never interpolated.

**Example:**

| Display | `k_auto` | Ladder | Rim (n = 2) | Outline (n = 2) | Gleam (n = 2 at `k` 1, else 1) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1280x720 (Steam Deck class) | 2 | 1 to 4 | 2, 4, 6, 8 px | same | 2, 2, 3, 4 px |
| 1920x1080 | 3 | 1 to 5 | 2, 4, 6, 8, 10 px | same | 2, 2, 3, 4, 5 px |
| 3840x2160 | 6 | 1 to 8 | 2, 4, ... 16 px | same | 2, 2, 3, ... 8 px |

At `k` 1 on 2160p the field is 3840x2160 art px (36 times the default 640x360 area) and a 2-px band is 2 screen px: legibility is OQ-PF-4 (PIPE OQ 4).

### D.11 Flash model

The flash model formula is defined as:

(a) region bound: `dY_region(1 s) = sum_e (a_e / A_region_min(k)) * Y_v * dAmp_e(1 s)`, required `dY_region <= FILTER_FLASH_MARGIN` **at every rung `k` of the ladder, evaluated at the smallest field** (`A_region_min(k) = W x H / (9 k^2)` in art px^2, the top rung `k_auto + 2` being the worst) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; if the margin is under 1 at a rung, the per-region cap on **breathing elements only**, `N_G_REGION(k) = max(0, floor((FILTER_FLASH_MARGIN x A_region_min(k) / Y_v - a_rim x dAmp - a_other x dAmp_other) / (a_gleam x dAmp)))` (`a_other x dAmp_other` = 20 x 100 x 0.06 = 120 for the non-breathing gleams), limits the simultaneous breathing gleams in one ninth of the picture, and the excess lies (newest first) take the honest path with `filter_draws_dropped`, never a lie without its gleam; it is a boundary guard that binds only at the smallest field

(b) patch-level pair bound: `Y_v * (ramp_slope * W + b_pair) < FLASH_LUM_DELTA`, `b_pair = d_b * (1 - cos(pi * W / T_min)) / 2`, `d_b = 1 - BREATH_FLOOR_PM / 1000`, `ramp_min = 1 / (FLASH_LUM_DELTA / Y_v - b_pair)`

(c) count bound: `N_b <= N_B_MAX = floor(FLASH_MAX_PER_S * T_min_s)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `a_e` | art px^2 | rim band about 600 (perimeter 300 x 2 px); gleam about 100 per surface; outline about 200 per subject | measured `[PLACEHOLDER]` | area of leak element `e` in the region |
| `A_region`, `A_region_min(k)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | art px^2 | 2 844 (640x360 at `k` 3) to 921 600 (largest field, a ninth of 3840x2160 at `k` 1); 25 600 is the default field at `k_auto` on 640x360 | LO-22 (cited): a ninth of the displayed picture at the current field; the field shrinks as `k` rises, so the smallest region is at the top rung `k_auto + 2` | region area |
| `Y_v` | float | 0.291 | `#B07BE6` in linear light | peak relative luminance a violet leak can add (alpha at most 1, peak channel `LEAK_MAX_CH` 0.85) |
| `dAmp_e(1 s)` | float | breath `d_b x pi / T_min = 0.471`; a needle gleam's wander and slope at most 0.06, a static-pair gleam breathes like the fringe (0.471); stage ramp at most `1 / STAGE_RAMP_S` = 0.125 | D.5, D.7, D.8 | greatest single-direction amplitude change in 1 s |
| `FILTER_FLASH_MARGIN` | float | 0.05 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 0.03 to 0.08 | Filter | design cap, half the PR-13 threshold |
| `FLASH_LUM_DELTA` | float | 0.10 (cited, PR-13) | Pipeline | opposing-pair magnitude that counts as a flash |
| `W` | float s | 1 | PR-13 | rolling window |
| `T_min`, `T_min_s` | ms, s | 4000 ms, 4 s (cited, ART 5.5) | art bible | shortest breath period |
| `d_b` | float | 0.6 at `BREATH_FLOOR_PM` 400 | derived | breath depth; the lie gleam breath uses `d_g = (AMP_MAX_PM - floor_eff) / 1000`, 0.6 at stages 0 to 1 and 0.75 at stage 3 (user ruling D74) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `ramp_slope` | float per s | `1 / STAGE_RAMP_S` | D.7 | the steepest stage-envelope slope (the latched `rate_s` never exceeds it) |
| `N_b` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | int | up to 5 at MVP (the rim when shipped, plus up to `LIES_MAX` 4 breathing gleams of static-pair and numeral-only STALE surfaces), up to 8 at VS-B (plus outlines up to `MAX_SEATS_TELL` 3, `[PLACEHOLDER]`) | derived | breathing elements per region |

**Output Range:** (a) at stage 3 with four lies and the rim, default field, taking the **worst case in which every one of the 24 gleam surfaces breathes** like a static-pair gleam (gleams on the static-pair classes now breathe, PF-15; the earlier text assumed they did not) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `((600 + 2400) / 25600) x 0.291 x 0.471 = 0.0161` at stages 0 to 1 (floor 400, breath depth 0.6), a margin of 3.1 against `FILTER_FLASH_MARGIN` 0.05 and of 6.2 against 0.10; at stage 3 the gleam floor has fallen to 250 and the static-pair gleam breath is 0.75 deep (D.5, user ruling D74), so `dAmp = 0.75 x pi / 4 = 0.589` and the same sum is `(3000 / 25600) x 0.291 x 0.589 = 0.0201`, a margin of 2.5 against 0.05 and of 5.0 against 0.10 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; the largest field makes it 36 times smaller (0.00045 at floor 400, 0.00056 at floor 250); the stage ramp alone is `(600 / 25600) x 0.291 x 0.125 = 0.00085`. The frame-mean sums divide by the whole picture, nine times smaller again. **Per rung (the smallest field)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `A_region_min` is 2 844 art px^2 (640x360, `k` 3, field 213x120), 6 400 (Steam Deck class 1280x720, `k` 4, field 320x180), 9 216 (1920x1080, `k` 5) and 14 400 (3840x2160, `k` 8). Two models are stated. The **`N_b` model** (the MVP composition: the rim plus up to 4 breathing static-pair or STALE gleams, `dAmp` 0.589 at stage 3, and the other 20 gleam surfaces non-breathing at 0.06) gives `dY_region = (600 x 0.589 + 4 x 100 x 0.589 + 20 x 100 x 0.06) x 0.291 / A_region_min` = 206.3 / `A_region_min`: **0.0725, 0.0322, 0.0224 and 0.0143** at the four top rungs (margins against `FILTER_FLASH_MARGIN` 0.05 of 0.69, 1.55, 2.2 and 3.5; against the true 0.10 threshold 1.4, 3.1, 4.5 and 7.0); at stages 0 to 1 (no rim, `dAmp` 0.471) it is 89.7 / `A_region_min` = 0.0316, 0.0140, 0.0097 and 0.0062. The earlier default-field figures (0.0161 and 0.0201) are the `k_auto` rung and overstated the margin at the top rungs. The **all-breathing worst case** (every one of the 24 surfaces and the rim breathing, 3 000 art px^2, `dAmp` 0.589) is the design-time upper bound: 514.2 / `A_region_min` = 0.181, 0.0803, 0.0558 and 0.0357; it exceeds 0.05 at the Deck top rung but is not the MVP composition (`N_b` is at most 5, below). Only the smallest field, **640x360 at `k` 3**, is under a margin of 1 on the `N_b` model (0.69 against 0.05, still 1.4 against 0.10): there the cap `N_G_REGION` limits the simultaneous **breathing** gleams (the rim and the non-breathing needle gleams are not capped), and it evaluates to 0 at stage 3 with the rim shipped (`0.05 x 2844 / 0.291 = 488.6`, less the rim 353.4 and the needle terms 120, leaves 15.2, under one gleam's 58.9), so a static-pair lie at stage 3 on a 640x360 window zoomed in twice takes the honest path with `filter_draws_dropped`; at the Deck top rung the cap is 10, at 1080p 18 and at 2160p 33 breathing gleams, all above the `N_b` of 4, so it never binds there; at stages 0 to 1 it is 7 at 640x360 `k` 3. Whether the ladder should offer `k` 3 on a 640x360 window at all is the Pipeline's (OQ-PF-45). (b) The pair magnitude of a breath inside any 1 s window is largest when the window is centred on a turning point: `b_pair = 0.6 x (1 - cos(pi / 4)) / 2 = 0.0879` of the band amplitude; conservatively adding the whole ramp slope to one side gives `0.291 x (0.125 + 0.0879) = 0.0620 < 0.10`. `ramp_min = 1 / (0.10 / 0.291 - 0.0879) = 3.91 s`. At stage 3 (user ruling D74) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* the gleam breath depth is 0.75, `b_pair = 0.75 x (1 - cos(pi / 4)) / 2 = 0.1098`, and the floor envelope adds a slope of `(400 - 250) / 1000 / 8 = 0.01875` per second to the ramp term (0.125 + 0.01875 = 0.1438): `0.291 x (0.1438 + 0.1098) = 0.0738 < 0.10`, `ramp_min = 1 / (0.10 / 0.291 - 0.1098) = 4.28 s`, still at or below the 6.5 s breath-rule floor of `STAGE_RAMP_S`. This conservative patch-level model passes at the adopted constants and **does not bind** `STAGE_RAMP_S` (the 6.5 s floor comes from the breath rule, D.7). (c) `N_B_MAX = floor(3 x 4) = 12` per region; a breath makes one opposing pair per period, 0.25 per second per element. **Reversal hazard:** a stage reversal inside 1 s would need two stage flips in 1 s; `STAGE_DWELL_RUNS` 25 (5 s, cited) keeps reversals at least 5 s apart. **Onset step** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the rise-only onset (0 to `floor_eff` on one gleam element, 0.291 x 0.4 = 0.116 at the patch level, 0.00045 at the region level) is a single-direction change and is the one intentional amplitude discontinuity; it cannot pair because the gleam breath starts at its trough (D.8) and ACTIVE falls are rate-limited at `R_AMP_ACT` (D.5). The model is the design-time check; the 60 s stage-3 scan at the largest field (zoom 1x on 2160p) is the proof. Every citation of PR-13 also cites HR-13 (no HUD element changes luminance faster than 3 Hz): the binding bound is the pair. The earlier draft derivation (6.41 s) used a breath depth of 0.3, a luminance of 0.5 and the change of a window starting at the trough; it is superseded. (orchestrator ruling; Open Questions.)

**Example:** the stage-3 breach composition at the default field: rim band 600 px^2 breathing, four lies with six gleam surfaces each at 100 px^2: `dY_region = 0.0161` with every gleam breathing (it was 0.0049 when gleams were taken as non-breathing); at `STAGE_RAMP_S` 8 the patch bound is 0.0620; the same scene at 2160p 1x has `A_region` 921 600 and `dY_region = 0.00045` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; at stage 3 with the floor at 250 the default-field figure is 0.0201 and the 2160p figure 0.00056 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

### D.12 Added-draw budget

The added-draw budget formula is defined as:

`D_added = [rim drawn] * 1 + N_tell_VSB * 3 + D_gleam + B_host` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; path 1 (instance parameters): `D_gleam = 0`, `B_host` 0 to 1 (the S-1b pass line: the lying hosts stay within +1 batch of the baseline, else path 1 is withdrawn); path 2 (MultiMesh overlay): `D_gleam = G_MM + [card open]` with `G_MM` the number of distinct (y-sort depth band, atlas page, light mask) groups among the lying hosts drawn this frame (one `MultiMeshInstance2D` each), 1 to 6, `B_host` 0; path 3 (one overlay per surface): `D_gleam = L_drawn * S_gleam(stage) + [card open]`, where only `L_drawn = min(L_max(stage), floor((D_FX_ADDED_MAX - rim - 3 N_tell_VSB - card) / S_gleam(stage)))` lies are drawn and every other lie takes the honest path (never a lie without its gleam, PF-15, PF-42) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

Requirement: `D_added <= D_FX_ADDED_MAX` (10; cited, art bible 8.6)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `L_max(stage)` | int | 0, 1, 2, 4 | `LIES_MAX` (cited, Sanity) | live lies per seat |
| `S_gleam(stage)` | int | 3, 5, 6 | PF-22 reach | gleam surfaces per lie if all are on screen: stage 1 needle edge, glass, readout glass; stage 2 plus glyph and housing; stage 3 plus sprite edge |
| `[card open]` | 0 or 1 | {0, 1} | IA-38 | at most one inspect card is open |
| `N_tell_VSB` | int | 0 at MVP; up to `MAX_SEATS_TELL` 3 | Co-op (VS-B) | class-(c) outlines: outline plus two beam edges per tell subject |
| `G_MM` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | int | 1 to `S_gleam(stage)` (6 at stage 3) | spike S-1c `[UNV]` | distinct (depth band, atlas page, light mask) groups among the lying hosts on screen: a `MultiMeshInstance2D` has one y-sort position, one texture, one material and one light mask, so one instance per group (the earlier "1 to 2" assumed a single group) |
| `B_host` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | int | 0 to 1 | spike S-1b `[UNV]` | extra draws from lying hosts breaking their batch runs: 0 on paths 2 and 3, at most 1 on path 1 (the S-1b pass line); a unique material per lying host would cost up to 2 draws per host (up to 48 for 24 hosts) and is excluded (PF-43) |
| `D_FX_ADDED_MAX` | int | 10 (cited, art bible 8.6; not a placeholder) | art bible | the fringe-plus-hallucination allowance inside `D_fx` |

**Output Range:** on the **instance-parameter path** (the gleam rides the lying instrument's own material, PF-42) `D_added` is 0 at stage 1 and 1 at stages 2 and 3 (plus `B_host` up to 1): inside 10 at every stage. The three paths compare as follows (surviving draws per stage, rim drawn from stage 2, one inspect card open on paths 2 and 3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*:

| Path | Stage 1 (1 lie) | Stage 2 (2 lies) | Stage 3 (4 lies) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 instance parameters | 0 draws, 1 lie | 1 draw (rim), 2 lies | 1 draw, 4 lies |
| 2 MultiMesh overlay (`G_MM` 2 best case; worst case `G_MM` = `S_gleam`: 3 + 1 = 4, 1 + 5 + 1 = 7, 1 + 6 + 1 = 8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | 2 + 1 = 3, 1 lie | 1 + 2 + 1 = 4, 2 lies | 1 + 2 + 1 = 4, 4 lies |
| 3 one overlay per surface, wanted | 3 + 1 = 4 | 2 x 5 + 1 + 1 = 12 | 4 x 6 + 1 + 1 = 26 |
| 3 one overlay per surface, surviving (budget 10) | 4 draws, 1 of 1 lie | 7 draws, **1 of 2** lies | 8 draws, **1 of 4** lies |

On path 3 the oldest lies survive (lowest `start_run`, then entry order, PF-46); the others are not drawn at all, count `filter_draws_dropped` (now counting lies) and give the Debug Overlay a `DEFECT_SILENT_LIE` with the counter as its explanation. The earlier cut order (drop the housing and glyph gleams, keep the needle's) removed 8 draws from 26 and still left 18 against the limit of 10, and left palette-silent lies; it is withdrawn. Bound facts: the count is bounded by `LIES_MAX`, **not by the field**, so zooming out to 1x multiplies nothing. VS-B: three tell subjects add 9 draws; `D_added` is then 1 + 9 = 10 at stage 3 on path 1, plus `B_host` up to 1 = 11 in the worst case, which is over the allowance by one and leaves nothing for any other fringe or hallucination draw *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, which is the PF-42 re-budget (OQ-PF-15). `D_filter` (the container pass) is 0 at MVP, 1 if route (a) ships; `D_FT_MAX` stays 38 (60 under route (a)).

**Example:** stage 3, four lies, rim shipped, card open, path 1: `D_added = 1`; path 2: `1 + 2 + 1 = 4`. Same scene on path 3: 26 wanted, so 1 lie is drawn with 8 draws and 3 take the honest path (`filter_draws_dropped` 3), and the lie that is drawn keeps GLEAM and its motion mode *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

### D.13 Presentation CPU

The presentation CPU formula is defined as:

`C_pf = N_vis * c_slot + N_dirty * c_honest + N_lie * (c_eval + f_chg * c_write)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

Requirement: `median(C_pf) <= CPU_BUDGET_MS`, `p99(C_pf) <= CPU_P99_MS` and `max(C_pf) <= CPU_MAX_MS`, over the scripted scenario SCN-PF-1 (10 minutes at stage 3 with four lies, a stage step and a restore)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `N_refs` | int | 0 to 80 | MVP slice: 5 compartments x 8 corruptible surfaces for the home ship plus an attached derelict `[PLACEHOLDER]` | corruptible refs, each with a **preallocated `Perceived` slot** created at tag registration and mutated in place (never reallocated per frame `[UNV]`; AC-PF-113 proves no two slots alias) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `N_vis` | int | 0 to 24 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | on-screen instruments | refs whose consumer reads its slot this frame (a property read, not an evaluation) |
| `N_dirty` | int | about `N_refs / RUN_FRAMES` = 6.7 on average; up to 80 at a restore or `hard_start` | derived | honest refs re-evaluated this frame: honest refs use a **push model**, evaluated only when the `perceived_dirty` notification marks them, not polled every frame |
| `N_lie` | int | 0 to 24 | `LIES_MAX x S_gleam` | refs under a live lie (evaluated every frame while on screen) |
| `f_chg` | float | 0 to 1 | derived | the fraction of lying refs whose written parameters changed this frame: a parameter is **written only on change**; 1 is the worst case |
